14:I[8669,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DateSidebar"]
15:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
16:T639,The main method is a parallel-corpus audit of tokenizers. The authors argue that non-parallel corpora confound language effects with topic, register, and sentence-length differences. By holding meaning constant across translations, differences in token counts can be attributed more directly to the interaction between language, script, morphology, and tokenizer vocabulary coverage. They evaluate 20 African languages against 11 current frontier and open tokenizers.

The metric design is careful. After Unicode NFC normalization, they compute word count, token count, character count, and UTF-8 byte count under a fixed convention shared across all languages. Token counts exclude BOS/EOS and other special tokens to avoid sequence-overhead artifacts. Word segmentation follows a uniform Unicode UAX-29 rule rather than language-specific heuristics, prioritizing comparability over linguistic tailoring. The paper then derives five metrics: fertility (tokens per word), premium relative to English, characters per token, bytes per token, and context efficiency under a fixed window size. Corpus-level aggregation uses sum-then-divide rather than mean-of-ratios, which is the right choice for stability.

A practical contribution is the translation of fertility into deployment-facing quantities: USD and local-currency costs, latency multipliers, and effective context-window erosion. This makes the tokenizer issue legible to system builders, not just NLP researchers. The inclusion of local-currency volatility in the cost model is also a realistic touch for the target deployment setting.17:T4d5,From the provided full-text content, the study evaluates 20 African languages and 11 tokenizers, with corpus-level aggregation and bootstrap confidence intervals. The language set spans multiple script families and morphological profiles, including Latin, Geʿez/Ethiopic, Arabic/Ajami, and N’Ko, which should make the analysis more informative than a generic multilingual average. The paper also instantiates the cost model in three deployment scenarios: bank customer service, clinical triage, and agricultural SMS assistance.

What is not fully visible in the provided text is the exact leaderboard of premiums, the worst-case language–tokenizer pairs, and the detailed ranking differences across tokenizers. So it would be unsafe to invent precise headline numbers. What is clear is the intended empirical claim: African languages incur a measurable and often substantial tokenization penalty relative to English, and that penalty compounds into higher cost, slower generation, and reduced effective context capacity. When reading the full paper, the most important results to inspect are the premium distributions by language and tokenizer, the script-family effects, and the relative context efficiency at the 128k window size.18:T45a,For a skim read, start with the introduction and the metrics section. Those two parts determine whether the measurement setup is credible. Focus on three design choices: why parallel corpora are necessary, why fertility is aggregated with sum-then-divide, and why bytes-per-token and context efficiency are reported alongside tokens-per-word. Then move to the main result tables or leaderboard plots to see which tokenizers are consistently better or worse for African languages and which scripts are most penalized. Finish with the cost model and deployment scenarios to judge whether the analysis maps cleanly to real production constraints.

A 25 to 35 minute read should be enough for the main thread. If reading more carefully, spend time on the appendix sections covering corpus construction, segmentation implementation, preprocessing edge cases, and bootstrap details. Measurement papers often live or die on these choices. One additional question worth checking is how sensitive the absolute fertility values are to the chosen word-segmentation standard for languages that do not align neatly with UAX-29.19:T8c7,The article starts from older learning-curve literature, including Amari, Hestness, and Rosenfeld, to make the point that power-law behavior predates modern LLMs. In that framing, scaling laws are empirical descriptions of how error changes with capacity, data, and compute. It then revisits Kaplan et al.: test loss scales as a power law with parameter count N, dataset size D, and compute C, and the joint form implies that overfitting is largely controlled by a ratio like N^(alpha/beta)/D. The important caveat is that Kaplan’s compute-optimal recommendation depends on the experimental range and fitting procedure, which pushed the optimum toward larger models trained on relatively fewer tokens.

The Chinchilla section is the core. It breaks down three fitting strategies. Method 1 fixes model size and varies the token budget to identify the best token count per model. Method 2 uses IsoFLOP profiles, comparing different N–D allocations under fixed total compute. Method 3 fits a parametric joint loss function directly. The article explains why these are not interchangeable: token sweeps reveal undertraining more directly, IsoFLOP studies align better with real compute allocation, and parametric fits are efficient but sensitive to functional assumptions and sampling coverage.

A particularly useful part is the discussion of fitting pitfalls. If experiments are concentrated in a narrow region, especially one that is effectively data-infinite or strongly model-limited, the fitted exponents can be systematically biased. If partially trained runs are treated as if they reflected compute-optimal behavior, the resulting recommendation will favor oversized, undertrained models. The toy simulation makes this concrete: even when the ground-truth law is known, limited coverage and noise can produce stable but wrong exponents. That is the real reconciliation between Kaplan and Chinchilla: not a simple contradiction, but different estimation procedures operating under different regimes.

The final section on why power laws appear is interpretive rather than formal. It argues that away from tiny-scale and saturation regimes, many error terms behave approximately scale-free, which makes log-log linearity empirically robust.1a:T625,This is a synthesis article rather than a new empirical paper, so the emphasis is on reinterpreting existing results and fitting procedures. It revisits Kaplan 2020: Transformer language models with roughly 768M to 1.5B non-embedding parameters, datasets from 22M to 23B tokens, trained with 3k-step warmup and cosine decay. One key Kaplan conclusion was N_opt ∝ C^0.73, implying that a 10x compute increase should scale model size by about 5.5x but tokens by only about 1.8x. The article highlights that this is exactly the recommendation later corrected by Chinchilla because it leaves models substantially undertrained.

For Chinchilla, the main takeaway is that compute-optimal scaling is much closer to growing parameters and training tokens in the same rough order, meaning smaller models trained on more data under a fixed compute budget. The excerpt provided here does not enumerate every number from the original Chinchilla study, but the experimental lesson is clear: once token sweeps or IsoFLOP comparisons are done carefully, many Kaplan-style large-model points are revealed to be undertrained, which shifts the optimum toward more tokens and fewer parameters.

The most informative “experiment” in the article is the toy simulation. It shows that the fitting pipeline itself can create bias: narrow sampling ranges, noise, and overly rigid parametric assumptions can all distort the inferred exponent and the downstream compute-optimal prescription. For anyone running internal scaling studies, that lesson matters more than any single fitted exponent.1b:T417,The paper first audits top-1 collapse warnings across 816 DLM PEFT configurations: 671 LLaDA-family configurations, 100 Dream-7B boundary configurations, and 45 MDLM-OWT 130M boundary configurations. The warning fires for every configuration, while the training loop records zero actual collapses at the 200-step horizon. The authors then explain the failure through saturation analysis. Top-1 token frequency is already 0.83±0.13 at step 0, every run is above 0.5, and the median run crosses 0.95 within four optimizer steps. The warning fire-step is not significantly associated with stability. The alternative signal is parameter-side: maximum LoRA gradient norm. A gradient routing probe shows that token CE gradients are only mildly concentrated, with Gini 0.29 and the largest evaluated token-position share at 1.5%, while LoRA parameter gradients are much more concentrated, with Gini 0.46 and one matrix carrying 63.0% of the gradient mass. This motivates using max-gradient as a family-local triage signal rather than a global threshold.1c:T540,The central result is the 816-configuration refutation: top-1 fires in 816/816 DLM PEFT runs, while actual collapse occurs in 0/816 runs, giving zero precision. Matched AR controls on Pythia 410M, 1B, 2.8B, 6.9B, and Qwen3.5-9B cover 360 audited configurations and also show 0/360 actual collapses, helping bound the claim. The saturation analysis reports step-0 top-1 frequency of 0.83±0.13, all runs above 0.5, median crossing 0.95 within four optimizer steps, and no significant relation between legacy fire-step and stability, with Mann–Whitney U p=0.20. For max-gradient, unstable configurations in LLaDA2.0-mini have a 3.23x higher median max-gradient norm than stable ones, with n=144, Bonferroni-corrected p=2.7e-7, and bootstrap 95% CI [2.76, 3.97]. On the method-comparison set, n=395, the ratio is 362x, with stable median 99.3 versus unstable median 35,960.4, pBonf=5e-21, CI [202, 779]. On a fixed 80/20 split of the 671-configuration LLaDA-family corpus, a threshold selected on the training split predicts held-out top-decile final-loss configurations with precision 0.68, recall 0.94, and F1 0.79. The all-positive top-1 baseline gives precision 0.13 on the same split. A split-bootstrap gives 95% CI [0.500, 0.947], above the baseline ceiling of 0.148. A step-k sweep shows max-gradient precision stabilizing around step 25.1d:T4df,The method, Block-GTQ, is built on TurboQuant-MSE but replaces uniform per-head bit allocation with integer bit allocation over RoPE 2D blocks inside each layer and KV head. The starting point is the RoPE attention logit q^T R_Δ k, which decomposes into a sum of 2×2 rotated block terms. This makes key-cache quantization naturally block-sensitive: quantization error in different blocks perturbs future logits by different amounts. The paper defines a label-free block energy score from Q/K activation statistics to estimate how important each block is for future query-key interactions. It then combines this score with the TQ-MSE empirical error law, where squared error scales roughly as 4^{-b}, to compute the marginal gain of assigning one more bit to a block. Under a fixed average-bit budget, bits are allocated greedily as integers. Blocks with the same assigned width are then encoded using the original TQ-MSE local quantizer, so the implementation remains lightweight. Values are still quantized with uniform TQ-MSE because V does not enter the RoPE key-logit computation. The paper also implements a packed-cache serving path so the compressed K/V codes are used in an actual inference pipeline rather than only in offline analysis.1e:T57c,The evaluation has three layers. First, mechanism validation: on a ten-model diagnostic panel, Block-GTQ is compared against uniform TQ-MSE at matched K-only budgets. At both 2 and 3 bits per dimension, it reduces per-layer RoPE-logit MAE by 32% to 80%, and wins all 367/367 layer comparisons at each budget. This indicates a broad structural improvement rather than gains from a few favorable layers. Second, long-context downstream tasks: on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct at K2V2, the six-task NIAH average rises from 70.6 to 97.4, and the eight-task LongBench-EN average rises from 36.87 to 53.31 relative to uniform-allocation TQ-MSE. Third, reasoning robustness: on DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, AIME 2024/2025 scores at K3V2 are 51.7/37.5 for Block-GTQ, close to fp16 at 54.2/37.9, while uniform TQ-MSE collapses to 0.0/0.0. That result is especially telling because it shows how low-bit key errors can destroy long-chain reasoning once they distort attention distributions, and how RoPE-aware allocation can prevent that failure. On the systems side, with Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and a K3V3 packed path, the method achieves 3.24× KV-cache compression at 128K context, reduces peak memory from 56.31GB to 19.85GB, and lowers single-request decode latency from 70.96ms to 52.95ms. At 256K and 512K, the fp16 baseline runs out of memory, while the packed path remains feasible at 33.42GB and 60.56GB peak memory.1f:T446,For a quick but useful read, focus on three parts. First, read the introduction and the derivation of RoPE-structured key-cache error. That is where the paper makes its central case that key quantization should preserve logits rather than just reconstruct vectors. Second, read the bit-allocation section carefully, especially how the energy score is defined, why it is label-free, and how the 4^{-b} rate law turns allocation into a greedy marginal-gain problem. Third, inspect the AIME and LongBench/NIAH result tables, since they show whether the improved logit fidelity actually preserves model function. If time is limited, the first half of the method section, the main result tables, and the packed-serving implementation notes are enough to capture the contribution in about 25 to 35 minutes. Two deeper follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: whether the learned energy profiles reflect layerwise frequency usage patterns from pretraining, and whether the same block-aware idea could be transferred to training-time regularization, distillation losses, or representation design.20:T877,方法核心有两部分。第一部分是 Progressive Curriculum Loss，用来解决深 look-ahead 训练不稳。论文不是对所有深度 k 的预测一视同仁地加权，而是给每个位置 t、每个深度 k 一个自适应权重 ω_{t,k}。这个权重由两类约束组成：Sequential Confidence Constraint 和 Retrospective Target Constraint。前者利用前面各层 look-ahead 预测的 softmax 置信度做累计约束，近似衡量“走到第 k 步这条路径是否还可信”；如果前面某一步已经低置信或偏离，后面更远的 supervision 会被压低。这样做的直接作用，是避免 distal target 在 proximal prediction 已经失败时继续制造 hallucinated gradients。后者则从回顾式目标一致性角度再做一次筛选，抑制那些和真实目标轨迹不一致的远端监督。两者叠加后，loss 会自然形成 easy-to-hard 的课程学习：先学稳近端，再逐步放开远端。

第二部分是 Confidence-Gated Dynamic Drafting，用于推理阶段动态决定实际 draft 多深。传统 MTP 常用固定 K，但固定 K 在文档解析里明显不合适，因为表格、公式、纯文本段落的局部熵差异很大。P-MTP 在每一步根据累计联合置信度判断是否继续向更深处 draft：如果前几步 token 的联合概率仍高，就继续扩展；一旦累计可靠性跌破阈值，就提前截断。这样优化的是 αK 而不是单独追求大 K，其中 α 是接受率。论文还强调 MTP module 需要足够轻量，否则额外前向开销 γ 会吃掉加速收益，因此他们采用 shared lightweight MTP module 递归地产生多个 look-ahead token，而不是为每个深度堆重头分支。

和已有方法相比，区别主要有三点。其一，不再使用 constant 或 static-decay loss weighting，而是显式让 loss 感知 trajectory divergence。其二，不再把 drafting depth 当成固定超参，而是把它做成基于置信度的在线决策。其三，场景上利用了文档解析“视觉证据强、输出分布尖锐”的特性，说明 MTP 在这类任务里比开放域文本生成更有条件做深 look-ahead。21:T896,The method has two main components. The first is Progressive Curriculum Loss, designed to stabilize deep look-ahead training. Instead of assigning a constant or hand-decayed weight to every prediction depth k at position t, the model uses an adaptive weight ω_{t,k}. This weight combines two constraints. The Sequential Confidence Constraint uses the softmax confidence of preceding look-ahead predictions to estimate whether the trajectory up to depth k is still trustworthy. If an earlier step is already uncertain or off-track, supervision for deeper predictions is down-weighted. This suppresses the classic failure mode where distal targets keep backpropagating through a path that has already collapsed. The Retrospective Target Constraint adds another filter based on consistency with the target trajectory, further reducing noisy supervision from unreliable deep predictions. Together, these terms create an implicit easy-to-hard curriculum: the model first learns stable short-horizon prediction and only gradually receives stronger supervision on deeper horizons.

The second component is Confidence-Gated Dynamic Drafting for inference. Standard MTP usually fixes the drafting depth K, but fixed K is suboptimal for document parsing because entropy varies sharply across layouts, tables, formulas, and plain text spans. P-MTP instead decides online how far to draft based on cumulative joint confidence. If the predicted path remains reliable, the model keeps extending the draft; if confidence drops below a threshold, it truncates early. This directly optimizes the useful quantity αK, where α is the acceptance rate, rather than maximizing K in isolation. The paper also keeps the MTP branch lightweight, using a shared module that recurrently produces multiple look-ahead predictions, so the extra overhead γ stays small enough for real wall-clock gains.

Relative to prior MTP work, the main differences are: trajectory-aware loss weighting instead of static weighting, adaptive drafting depth instead of fixed-depth drafting, and an explicit exploitation of the document-parsing regime where visual evidence makes token distributions sharper than in open-ended text generation.22:T639,From the full text provided, the main empirical claim is clear: P-MTP reaches up to 5× inference speedup on document parsing with negligible accuracy loss. The paper attributes this to the combination of stable deep look-ahead training and adaptive drafting at inference, rather than either component alone.

The most important experiments are likely organized around four questions. First, comparisons against standard next-token prediction, vanilla fixed-depth MTP, and static loss-weighting variants, to show that progressive weighting is necessary. Second, scaling studies over look-ahead depth K, where baseline MTP should show collapsing acceptance as K grows, while P-MTP remains usable at deeper horizons. Third, ablations on dynamic drafting, showing that even if a model can be trained with deep K, a fixed K still wastes speedup because local difficulty varies. Fourth, overhead analysis of the lightweight MTP module, since theoretical speedup only matters if the extra branch cost remains low.

The hard numbers visible in the provided text are limited to 'up to 5× speedup with negligible impact on parsing accuracy.' The excerpt does not include full benchmark tables, model size, training scale, acceptance-rate curves, or detailed latency breakdowns. When reading the original paper, the most important missing numbers to extract are: backbone VLM size, parameter share of the MTP module, acceptance rate α at different K, wall-clock throughput rather than token-level proxy metrics, and gains across different document types such as tables, formulas, and long text blocks.23:T501,This is a skim-level paper, but the method section deserves careful attention. Start with Figure 1 in the introduction, because it frames the two real problems cleanly: unstable deep supervision and inefficiency of fixed-depth drafting. Then go straight to the formulas for Progressive Curriculum Loss. The key questions are how cumulative confidence is computed, whether gradients are stopped through the weighting path, and what exactly the retrospective consistency term compares against. Those details determine whether the method is a principled credit-assignment mechanism or mostly a heuristic reweighting trick.

Next, read the algorithm for Confidence-Gated Dynamic Drafting and check how thresholds are chosen, whether calibration is needed, and how hidden states are reused across depths. In the experiments, focus on three plots or tables if available: training stability versus depth, acceptance/speedup versus depth, and the accuracy-latency trade-off. Around 30-40 minutes should be enough to capture the main idea. If the goal is to judge transferability to general LM training, also inspect the supplementary derivation of speedup and any failure cases on complex layouts, especially whether the dynamic policy often falls back to shallow drafting in hard regions.24:T5c2,On provenance/topicality, the best off-the-shelf relevance scores reach 0.73–0.97 top-1 accuracy; NLI overlaps with the best confidence intervals on MS MARCO and HotpotQA but is unreliable on ASQA at 0.585. On VitaminC fact-checking, entailment-trained models reach 0.81–0.89 AUROC, compared with roughly 0.60 for surface metrics. The strongest result is on generated-answer attribution. Across the four AttributionBench source datasets, with n=1610, no audited automatic scorer is consistently near-best. Metric rankings invert between AttributedQA and LFQA, with Kendall tau = -0.64 and p = 0.031. A raw MNLI scorer reaches 0.90 AUROC on AttributedQA but drops to 0.53 on LFQA, while BERTScore reaches 0.91 on LFQA. On the independent long-form HAGRID set, n=2150, the same NLI scorer is again best at 0.80, so the LFQA failure is not explained by long-form answers alone. MiniCheck has the lowest mean leave-one-dataset-out regret among the eight automatic scorers, 0.044 with CI [0.014, 0.072], but still leaves measurable regret. A naive best-on-average selector has 0.172 regret. A small LLM-annotation probe with Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.4 on 160 examples shows high inter-model agreement but only moderate, dataset-dependent agreement with humans. A GRPO reward probe at 3B and 7B changes outputs substantially while leaving QA-EM, ROUGE-L, and citation count essentially unchanged, showing that coarse deterministic metrics can miss grounding-related behavior shifts.25:T499,Agora is a benchmark plus a construction/evaluation pipeline. Task formulation: each question is paired with a fixed, closed collection C; the agent can only use tools to navigate/read within C and must output a single, uniquely verifiable numeric answer.
Key design choices:
1) Archive-groundedness via frozen collections (no web), ensuring reproducibility and preventing drift.
2) Scale that forces deliberate exploration: collections are far larger than context windows (372M tokens total), ruling out brute-force stuffing.
3) Cross-domain, heterogeneous corpora: 8 domain collections with diverse formats and conventions.
4) Deterministic scoring: numeric answers avoid LLM-as-judge.
Dataset construction (as described): an agentic pipeline gathers authentic documents, synthesizes cross-document multi-hop questions, applies leakage-preventing obfuscation, filters for difficulty (to require real search/verification/computation), and uses human verification.
Evaluation harness: a minimal agent interface (mini-swe-agent) exposing only a bash tool, aiming to control for scaffolding differences and attribute variance to model reasoning, evidence selection, and tool use.26:T470,Two-part evaluation protocol:
1) Latent–concept matching (correlational): given binary human annotations Y (attributes) and SAE latent activations z_l across samples, binarize latents and match them to attributes. The key design is allowing many-to-one mappings (one concept ↔ a coalition of latents) to handle feature splitting. They propose a coalition-based objective and a Fully-Binary Matching Pursuit (FBMP) procedure that greedily builds a latent set per attribute to optimize a set-level F-score (they recommend F0.5 to emphasize precision).
2) Targeted Attribute Perturbation Alignment (interventional): correlational matching can be confounded by co-occurrence, so they introduce paired-image benchmarks where exactly one attribute changes. They build synCUB and synCOCO as synthetic minimal-pair datasets. TAPAScore then checks whether the matched latent set for an attribute responds selectively and in the correct direction on these targeted perturbations.
They additionally run sanity checks showing that several existing matching metrics can behave poorly under simple controls, while FBMP + TAPAScore is more robust.27:T49f,Wan-Streamer uses a single Transformer over interleaved multimodal input and output tokens, coordinated by block-causal attention. At each streaming step, newly observed audio-visual units are immediately consumed, and generated audio-visual units are committed back into the interaction history. This lets the model condition future behavior on both the user and its own previous actions. The full stack is causal: audio and video VAEs, audio-visual encoders, audio and video decoders, and the temporal Transformer are all designed for streaming. The generation path avoids a hidden text bottleneck; speech and visual latents are generated from the same persistent state. At inference time, the model is scheduled as a thinker-performer pipeline. The thinker encodes current user observations, runs the short token-causal Transformer pass, predicts language, and updates KV-cache. The performer receives the new KV context and runs the flow-matching solver for the next audio-visual latent unit. The previous latent unit is returned to the thinker, decoded, and emitted. This keeps a unified causal state while overlapping perception, decoding, latent generation, and communication.28:T863,EVA builds an autoregressive Thought-Visual sequence. Given an image and a query, the model generates alternating textual thoughts T_i and latent visual states A_i: (T1, A1), (T2, A2), and so on, until it decides enough evidence has been gathered and outputs the final answer. Several design choices matter here. First, Latent_slot is continuous, not a symbolic tool command. It is trained to approximate task-relevant visual features, effectively standing in for the visual result that an external tool would have returned. Second, the model does not regress to all image features uniformly. It uses saliency-weighted supervision to focus the latent state on semantically relevant regions for the current question and reasoning step, reducing interference from irrelevant background. Third, the number of slots is adaptive rather than fixed, so the model can allocate more internal visual exploration to harder examples. To support this, the authors build EVA-SFT-230K, where each sample contains a random and context-dependent number of latent slots.

Training is staged. Stage 1 teaches the model to generate Latent_slots. Stage 2 teaches the model to actually use them in downstream reasoning, rather than treating them as inert placeholders. Stage 3 applies GSPO-style RL to improve exploratory visual reasoning. The most informative part of the paper is the RL analysis. The authors identify a “transition window” immediately after each latent slot, where policy deviation becomes much larger than at other token positions. These tokens are where the model has to translate the latent state into subsequent textual reasoning, so their distribution shifts sharply under joint optimization. To stabilize training, they propose D-GSPO: the policy objective is computed only on discrete text probabilities, while gradients are still allowed to flow back through the latent slot; in parallel, a stronger localized KL penalty is applied specifically to the transition-window tokens. The trade-off is clear: preserve exploration through latent states, but avoid letting RL aggressively rewrite both the continuous and discrete channels at once.29:T53f,The experimental evidence falls into three buckets. First, EVA consistently improves over pure textual CoT and explicit tool-invocation baselines on multiple visual reasoning benchmarks, suggesting that latent visual states provide useful intermediate evidence rather than just extra model flexibility. Second, EVA is more inference-efficient than conventional tool-use pipelines across resolutions, because it avoids repeated external cropping/detection and visual re-encoding; the advantage grows with higher-resolution inputs. Third, the stability analysis is central: naive joint GSPO over latent and text leads to much larger KL divergence, gradient norms, and training volatility in the transition window, while D-GSPO suppresses these instabilities and preserves the performance gains.

The most useful ablation-level takeaways are: adaptive slot counts outperform fixed slot counts; saliency-weighted alignment is better than uniform regression to full-image features; and the three-stage curriculum is better than one-shot end-to-end training. The abstract does not provide full numeric tables, and the provided full-text excerpt does not include all result tables, so it would be unsafe to invent exact gains. The paper’s main evidence is the combination of accuracy, latency, and stability, not a single headline benchmark number.2a:T809,TuringViT has three main components. The first is architectural. It introduces Turing Linear Attention (TLA) as the dominant attention operator, replacing most softmax-attention layers with a linear-complexity alternative. TLA is not just a generic linearized attention block. It adds sequence-length-aware normalization and an input-dependent output gate, aiming to remain stable when the number of visual tokens varies widely while preserving local and high-frequency visual responses. The model does not remove full attention entirely; instead, it inserts a small number of standard softmax MHA layers periodically for explicit token-token interaction and global routing. This hybrid design is pragmatic: linear attention handles most of the cost, while sparse full attention recovers some of the expressivity.

The second component is data: VISTA-Curation. For image-language data, the pipeline filters low-quality images, generates multiple caption candidates, and then selects the best one using relative image-text scoring plus textual informativeness ranking. For video-language data, it decomposes raw videos into compact clips, samples representative frames, filters clips by semantic and motion consistency, and fuses local frame details with global temporal semantics into better captions. The key idea is not just “more cleaning,” but converting noisy image-text and video-text corpora into more grounded and discriminative supervision.

The third component is training. Instead of pretraining at a fixed resolution and later adapting to dynamic resolution, TuringViT is trained with variable resolutions and aspect ratios from the start. To keep this affordable, the first 80% of training caps the maximum image size at 512, and the later stage removes that cap and introduces larger images and video data. The logic is straightforward: learn robust generic visual representations early, then spend the expensive part of the budget on high-resolution and temporal modeling later. The linear-attention backbone makes this schedule feasible.2b:T5c2,The experiments are designed to support three claims. First, under similar or lower resource budgets, TuringViT matches or exceeds strong open-source ViT baselines, suggesting that the hybrid linear-attention design does not significantly compromise representation quality. Second, VISTA-Curation delivers strong data efficiency: the paper claims it can outperform leading open-source ViT baselines using only about 10% of the data scale, which implies that better supervision density can offset a large amount of raw data volume. Third, under dynamic resolution and long visual sequences, TuringViT shows a much flatter latency curve than standard softmax-based ViTs, with scaling closer to linear as sequence length grows. That matters for high-resolution images, multi-image inputs, and video.

The excerpt also indicates a scaling-law analysis: performance improves in a predictable way as curated data scale increases, suggesting the model is still far from saturation. The most useful ablations to inspect are likely: pure softmax vs pure linear vs hybrid attention; with and without VISTA-Curation; fixed-resolution pretraining vs native dynamic-resolution pretraining; and the effect of the progressive resolution schedule. The provided text does not include full result tables or exact numbers, so it would be unsafe to state precise gains. The paper’s central claim is the combination of lower cost, less data, dynamic-resolution support, and strong performance.2c:T933,The method is a two-pass video understanding framework with two key behavioral designs. In the first pass, the model watches the full video at low temporal and spatial fidelity. Visual and audio tokens are interleaved and fed to the LLM together with the question. The output is structured: an initial answer A^(1), a short reasoning trace R^(1), and a temporal localization prediction T = [t_start, t_end]. The localization acts as a policy decision that determines which segment will be revisited at higher fidelity.

In the second pass, the model re-encodes only the localized segment using higher frame rate or resolution, while retaining the first-pass global context and the interaction trace from the first pass. It then produces a refined final answer. The two main design choices are there to make this new re-watch behavior compatible with the pretrained model’s existing answering prior.

The first is re-answer. The authors argue that an instruction-tuned video LLM has an answer-first tendency: given a question and a coarse video view, it naturally tries to answer immediately. Forcing the model into a strict locate-then-reason pattern creates a mismatch with that prior and can destabilize learning. Re-answer avoids this by letting the model answer first in the initial pass, then revise the answer after re-watching. This makes temporal localization an add-on capability attached to the existing QA behavior rather than a replacement for it.

The second is re-ask. Under causal attention, question tokens from the first pass cannot directly attend to newly observed high-fidelity frames in the second pass. That weakens question adherence exactly when the model is supposed to use the new evidence. The fix is simple: inject the query again during the second pass so the question can directly interact with the revisited segment. Mechanistically, this matters because otherwise the second pass mostly extends the first-pass hidden-state trajectory instead of re-conditioning on fresh evidence.

Training-wise, the paper avoids CoT cold-start and CoT-based SFT. It starts from an instruction-tuned base model and directly optimizes re-watch behavior with DAPO-based RL. The important point is not the specific RL label, but the framing: 'where to look' is treated as a learnable policy, not as an intermediate supervised label sequence.2d:T5ae,From the provided full-text excerpt, the main empirical claim is that video-SALMONN-R^3 achieves stronger results on six benchmarks at lower computational cost, while avoiding CoT cold-start annotations. The gains are attributed to three pieces working together: RL-learned re-watch behavior, re-answer preserving the base model’s original answering prior, and re-ask restoring question-to-evidence interaction in the second pass.

The most important comparisons should include: a single-pass video-LLM baseline, prior re-watch systems that depend on multiple models or CoT-SFT, an ablation without re-answer to test whether the base capability degrades, and an ablation without re-ask to measure the value of query reinjection. A complete experimental section should also report temporal localization quality, cost-benefit trade-offs across video lengths and resolutions, and token-budget allocation between the first and second pass.

The excerpt does not include benchmark names, absolute scores, model size, sampling rates, or RL training scale, so the exact improvement margins cannot be reproduced here. What should be checked in the original paper is whether the claimed efficiency is measured in total visual tokens, FLOPs, or wall-clock latency; what fraction of the video is revisited at high fidelity; how much compute is saved relative to processing the full video at high fidelity; and whether RL training cost is accounted for separately.2e:T466,Read this one with a specific lens. Start from the introduction, but focus on the critique of the two existing re-watch routes: multi-model communication overhead and the side effects of CoT supervision. Then inspect the overall method figure to understand exactly what enters and exits each pass. Once that is clear, the most informative parts are the re-answer and re-ask designs.

If time is limited, prioritize three things. First, the RL objective and reward design: how temporal localization and final QA quality are coupled, and whether there are auxiliary rewards for formatting or valid time spans. Second, the ablation table, especially removing re-answer and re-ask, because that reveals whether the gains come from real mechanism design or simply from spending more compute in a second pass. Third, the cost analysis, since the efficiency claim is central. A skim should take around 35-45 minutes. If the goal is to extract lessons for the pretrain-to-posttrain interface, also inspect failure cases, especially whether the second pass truly corrects wrong first-pass answers or mostly reinforces the initial bias.2f:T443,The paper formulates compositional generation over a perturbation space A and an outcome space X. Only a subset of conditions is observed, and the goal is to sample from an unseen condition whose density can be written as a geometrically weighted product of source densities. At the clean-data level, such a geometric product corresponds to a linear combination of score functions, which explains the common heuristic of adding conditional scores during denoising. The paper then separates two error sources. The first is inference-time approximation error: after forward noising, the clean-distribution score identity no longer holds exactly. Particle-based methods such as Feynman-Kac Correctors attempt to reduce this error by tracking importance weights along the reverse process. The second is score estimation error: typical samples from the composed target may lie in low-density regions of each source distribution, where the learned score is poorly constrained. The main claim is that this second error often dominates, and FKC-style correction can amplify it rather than repair it.30:T415,ZONOS2 uses a decoder-only Transformer MoE backbone with 8B total parameters and roughly 900M active parameters per forward pass. Audio is represented with DAC, an RVQ-based neural codec; the report uses 9 codebooks. The model autoregressively predicts audio tokens conditioned on text and previous audio tokens. A key systems-modeling choice is the delay pattern for codebook generation. Codebook j is delayed by j frames, turning within-frame RVQ dependencies into sequence dependencies. This lets the model condition later codebooks of a frame on earlier codebooks of the same frame, instead of treating codebooks as conditionally independent. Text is represented as UTF-8 bytes rather than phonemes, which avoids language-specific grapheme-to-phoneme conversion and out-of-vocabulary handling. The system also supports zero-shot voice cloning, speaking-rate control, and an optional quality mode. The voice-cloning setup does not require speaker labels during training and does not require a transcript of the prompt audio at inference time.31:T5c2,Four core findings emerge: (1) SFT, RFT, and RIFT produce nearly colinear weight deltas (cosine ≥0.97, median per-layer top-1 principal angle of ~7 degrees), with GSM8K accuracies ranging 87.3-88.2% and no statistically significant pairwise differences per exact McNemar’s test (p≥0.15); (2) DFT, a one-line modification of SFT that down-weights high-confidence tokens, produces a more distinct weight update than all three reward-filtered SFT variants; (3) Offline GRPO introduces a measurable orthogonal update component (67% globally, rising to ~80% in late layers), but remains in the same loss basin as SFT/RIFT with no sharp performance drop under linear weight interpolation; (4) DPO occupies a near-orthogonal weight subspace with higher effective rank and sharp linear mode barriers relative to other methods, reaching 93.5% pass@1 on GSM8K (vs. ~87-88% for other offline methods) and top performance on AIME26, though this gap is confounded by DPO’s 10x lower learning rate and ~40x smaller training set size in the protocol, making it impossible to fully separate effects of the loss formulation from optimizer settings. All core geometric patterns and the relative DPO accuracy edge replicate on Llama-3.2-3B. Supplementary experiments show on-policy RL methods (Online GRPO, DAPO) produce orthogonal weight updates and maintain 93-94% GSM8K accuracy (matching the base model), avoiding the ~7 point accuracy drop observed with offline SFT-style methods.32:T592,The method is called blockwise policy-drift gating. For each sampled response token, the paper computes the old-current log-probability shift: the difference between the current student’s log-probability and the behavior student’s log-probability on the same sampled token. This is not a full KL estimate; it is a sampled-path drift signal. Using token-level importance ratios such as exp(d_t) is a natural baseline, but the paper argues that it is too noisy. Instead, it aggregates drift at an intermediate granularity. Two variants are used: Block64-Gate, which averages shifts over fixed 64-token blocks, and NewlineSpan-Gate, which aggregates over newline-delimited spans with heuristics for merging or splitting spans by length. The aggregated score is passed through a detached soft gate and mean-normalized over valid response tokens, then broadcast back to all positions in the region as a multiplicative weight on the existing OPD position loss. Three design choices matter. First, the gate is detached, so the model cannot optimize the gate itself; it only changes how much each position contributes to the base loss. Second, teacher targets are unchanged, so the same gating family can be applied both to sampled-token OPD and to Teacher-TopK/LSM. Third, blockwise aggregation is a variance-locality trade-off: sequence-level weighting is too coarse for long traces, while token-level weighting is too unstable.33:T438,First, a domain-agnostic Domain-Specific Language (DSL) is proposed to convert free-form natural language reasoning traces into explicit state transition sequences. It makes step dependencies explicit, compiles quantitative content into executable expressions, and constrains non-formalizable semantic inferences to a small set of universal deduction schemas (direct entailment, modus ponens, transitivity, case analysis). A hybrid verifier then splits checks into two categories: deterministic checks for no forward dependency references, consistency between computation results and state updates, satisfiability of mechanizable constraints, and alignment between conclusions and declared goals; and scoped structured LLM audits for non-mechanizable semantic parts (natural language constraints, commonsense inferences). An iterative repair loop is implemented: generate CoT → formalize to DSL → verify → return localized error diagnostics if invalid → revise only problematic regions → re-verify, with no additional training or domain-specific demonstrations required.34:T545,The paper has two parts: characterization and mitigation. In the characterization part, pigeonholing is operationalized through three behaviors. One is mistake repetition: the model copies or extends an incorrect answer from context, especially in verifiable domains such as math and coding. Another is stance flipping: the model changes its original answer to align with an incorrect in-context claim. The third is mode collapse: even when the in-context example is not wrong but merely one valid option among many, the model becomes much less likely to explore alternatives and instead reproduces the provided pattern. This broadens the phenomenon from simple accuracy loss to distributional narrowing.

The mitigation strategy is synthetic error augmentation. Instead of changing the architecture, the authors inject erroneous conversational contexts into RLVR and DPO training data, so the model is explicitly optimized to recover when the conditioning history is already corrupted. This is the key distinction from vanilla RLVR or vanilla DPO, which are trained on cleaner or more standard preference-style data. The paper’s argument is that the failure is not just task incompetence; it is misconditioning under bad context. Therefore the training distribution must include corrupted histories if one wants robust recovery at inference time.35:T567,The empirical coverage is broad. The study evaluates frontier closed models including GPT-4.1, Sonnet 4.6, and Gemini 2.5 Pro, plus seven open-weight models in the 2B–9B range. Tasks span 10 settings, including coding, math, persuasion, and open-ended user interactions, with real-user queries drawn from PRISM, Infinity Chat 100, and Community Alignment. Additional ablations are run on six benchmarks covering logical, commonsense, and social reasoning.

The headline results are clear. Even strong frontier models degrade substantially under erroneous context: around 38% performance drop in math and 40% in coding. The degradation is often monotonic with the number of turns, which suggests cumulative context poisoning rather than isolated prompt brittleness. On the mitigation side, RLVR with synthetic errors improves recovery by 43%–60% over vanilla RLVR on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution tasks. DPO with synthetic errors improves by 34%–35% over vanilla DPO. A key negative result is that vanilla RLVR and vanilla DPO do not reliably fix mistake pigeonholing at test time.

Another strength of the experiments is that they do not only report task accuracy. They also track repeated mistakes, stance changes, and reduced answer diversity, which makes the phenomenon more convincing as a distributional failure mode rather than a narrow benchmark artifact.36:T452,The contribution is a structured design space for inference-time cross-lingual exploration rather than a single new algorithm. The core move is to treat “reasoning language / answering language” as exploration axes: translate the query into one or multiple candidate languages, run independent reasoning paths in each language to elicit parametric facts, then aggregate candidates back into the target language.
They claim four inherent dimensions governing cross-lingual exploration (details are in the main method section; from the provided content and cited prior work, these dimensions plausibly include: reasoning vs answering language, pivot vs non-pivot routing, which prompt components are translated, and multi-path sampling/aggregation).
A key stance is to move beyond an English-only pivot: some culturally/localized facts are more accessible in their native language. Aggregation resembles cross-lingual self-consistency: multiple linguistic paths produce candidates, and a selection/voting mechanism increases the probability of retrieving the correct entity while reducing hallucinations.37:T48d,First, a taxonomy of 18 fine-grained misalignment indicators is constructed, each corresponding to a unitary cognitive process (e.g., strategic omission, fact fabrication) and meeting three design criteria: single representational feature (learnable by a linear classifier), low false positive rate in benign reasoning, and high coverage across diverse misalignment behaviors. A meta-plan-guided automated pipeline is then designed to generate multi-turn training conversations containing each indicator, paired with hard negatives and benign control samples, for training linear probes (lightweight linear classifiers trained on intermediate model activations to detect specific internal representation patterns). Independent linear classifiers are trained on cached intermediate layer activations for each indicator. To reduce false positives, a cascade monitoring system combining white-box (probe-based) and black-box (LLM judge) checks is introduced. For rigorous generalization evaluation, an out-of-distribution test suite is built by combining automated behavioral elicitation and established misalignment benchmarks with natural misaligned model paper addresses online data mixing for LLM pretraining: how to adapt domain sampling probabilities during training based on the model’s current state, instead of relying on a fixed mixture or a single optimization signal.","importance":"The useful idea is to frame data mixture scheduling as a continuous-control problem with multiple feedback signals. This is relevant to pretraining data research because mixture quality often determines convergence speed and capability allocation. The paper is worth skimming rather than reading in full unless the implementation details are needed. The open question is operational cost. A scheduler that depends on gradient influence signals, actor-critic training, and tuned reward weights may be harder to deploy in large-scale training than static recipes or proxy-model approaches.","technical_approach":"HDS formulates LLM pretraining as an MDP. The environment is the training run. The state vector represents current performance, learning velocity, and internal stability. The action is a probability distribution over data domains for future sampling. The policy is trained with Soft Actor-Critic, which fits the continuous action space and uses entropy regularization for exploration. The actor and critic are compact Transformer-like networks. The main design choice is the reward: it combines a data-quality term, a gradient-driven inter-domain influence term inspired by gradient alignment in AC-ODM, and a model-centric stability term based on layer weight norms. Compared with static approaches such as DoReMi, DoGE, and RegMix, HDS changes the mixture throughout training. Compared with ODM-style bandit rewards or AC-ODM’s narrower gradient-alignment reward, it uses a broader set of signals.","experiments":"The reported main experiment pretrains Pythia-1B on The Pile. HDS reaches the final validation perplexity of the best reported online baseline with 22% fewer iterations, and with 57% fewer iterations than the static The Pile Weights baseline. On MMLU 0-shot, it reports a 7.2% gain over the best reported baseline. The important checks are the ablations: how much each reward component contributes, whether SAC itself matters compared with DDPG or bandit-style policies, whether the extra scheduler cost is included, and whether the learned schedule remains stable across model sizes and domain partitions.","reading_guide":"A 20- to 30-minute skim is enough for most pretraining readers. Focus on the MDP definition, especially the exact state features, action parameterization, and reward equations. Then inspect Figure 1 for convergence behavior and look for reward ablations and domain-weight trajectories. The practical question is whether the scheduler can be converted into a low-frequency controller or distilled into an offline mixture schedule. If the method requires frequent extra gradient computations, its usefulness at larger scale will depend on careful systems accounting.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"用 SAC 做在线 data mixture 思路新，但奖励设计决定成败；先看 §3 reward 定义和 ablations，再判断能否在内部复现。","en":"Using SAC for online data mixing is a fresh angle, but the reward design is make-or-break; read §3 and the ablations first before deciding whether to replicate internally."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Chenhao Dang","Jing Ma","Mingjie Liao"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["data mixture","online data scheduling","reinforcement learning","pre-training curriculum","SAC"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24133","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.24133","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.24408","title":"Natural Identifiers for Privacy and Data Audits in Large Language Models","abstract":"Assessing the privacy of large language models (LLMs) presents significant challenges. In particular, most existing methods for auditing differential privacy require the insertion of specially crafted canary data during training, making them impractical for auditing already-trained models without costly retraining. Additionally, dataset inference, which audits whether a suspect dataset was used to train a model, is infeasible without access to a private non-member held-out dataset. Yet, such hel","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-23T10:45:11Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2606_24408.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决 LLM 隐私与数据审计中的两个实际障碍：差分隐私审计通常需要训练前插入 canary，无法对已训练模型做低成本 post-hoc 审计；dataset inference 又需要与可疑数据同分布的非成员 held-out 集，现实中很难获得。","importance":"对预训练数据研究的价值在于，它提供了一种更可操作的训练数据成员审计接口。论文提出 natural identifiers，利用训练语料中自然出现的结构化随机字符串，例如哈希、短链接、加密货币地址。这类字符串有明确生成规则，因此可以生成同分布的非成员样本。这个思路把“必须预先埋 canary”变成“利用语料中天然存在的随机标识符”，使已发布模型的数据使用审计和隐私审计更可行。它也提醒预训练数据治理：代码库、论坛和网页中的随机标识符不是普通噪声，可能成为可检测的训练数据指纹。","technical_approach":"论文的核心技术是 Natural Identifiers，简称 NIDs。NIDs 是由已知机制生成、在自然语料中真实存在的随机字符串。由于格式分布可复现，审计者可以针对某种 NID 类型生成大量同分布的 non-member 样本。对于 DP 审计，论文改造 Steinke et al. 的 one-run auditing 框架，用训练集中自然出现的 NID 作为事后 canary，再用生成的同分布 NID 构造审计集，从而不需要重新训练模型。对于 dataset inference，流程是先在可疑数据集里识别 NID 类型，再生成同类型 held-out 集，然后用已有 DI 方法比较模型在 suspect NIDs 和 generated non-member NIDs 上的行为差异。与人工注入 canary 的方法相比，这个方案不要求修改预训练数据，也不要求访问私有同分布验证集。","experiments":"实验使用训练数据已知的开源模型和语料做受控验证，包括 Pythia 系列与 The Pile，以及 OLMo 与 Dolma。结果显示，NIDs 可以支持 post-hoc DP auditing，并且相比 Steinke et al. 的原始审计设置给出更强的隐私参数下界，同时降低所需 NID canary 数量。Dataset inference 实验表明，在包含 NID 的多个数据子集上可以判断其是否进入训练集，并报告没有 false positives。论文强调该方法适用于任何包含可识别 NID 类型的 suspect dataset，但有效性依赖 NID 的自然出现频率、格式生成器是否准确、以及模型是否对这些随机标识符产生了可测的记忆信号。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 25–35 分钟。重点看 Introduction 中对 DP auditing、membership inference、dataset inference 三者关系的梳理；方法部分看 NID 的定义、生成同分布 non-member 的条件，以及如何嵌入 Steinke et al. 和 Maini et al. 的审计流程；实验部分重点看 Pythia/Pile 与 OLMo/Dolma 的设置、false positive 控制、sample complexity 对比。若只关心预训练数据治理，可直接读 NID 类型统计和 dataset inference 结果。","authors_note":"CISPA Helmholtz Center for Information Security；作者包括 Lorenzo Rossi、Bartłomiej Marek、Franziska Boenisch、Adam Dziedzic。"},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses two practical blockers in LLM privacy and data audits. Formal DP audits usually require canaries to be inserted before training, which prevents cheap audits of already-trained models. Dataset inference needs an IID non-member held-out set for the suspect data, which is often unavailable in real cases.","importance":"The contribution is mainly about pretraining-data auditability. The paper identifies naturally occurring structured random strings as audit handles inside real corpora. Examples include cryptographic hashes, shortened URLs, and cryptocurrency addresses. Because their generation process is known, auditors can synthesize non-member strings from the same distribution. This creates a post-hoc alternative to injected canaries and makes dataset-level training membership tests more practical for pretrained LLMs.","technical_approach":"The method introduces Natural Identifiers, or NIDs. A NID is a naturally occurring random-looking string with a well-defined format and generator. The known generator lets the auditor create unlimited non-member samples that match the format distribution of the suspected member strings. For DP auditing, the paper adapts the one-run auditing framework of Steinke et al. by treating naturally present NIDs as retroactive canaries and generating matched audit strings after training. For dataset inference, it identifies NID types inside a suspect dataset, generates held-out NIDs of the same type, and applies DI procedures that compare model behavior on suspect strings versus generated non-members. The key difference from canary-injection approaches is that no retraining and no modification of the pretraining corpus are required.","experiments":"The empirical validation uses open models with known training corpora, including the Pythia suite trained on The Pile and OLMo trained on Dolma. The results show that NIDs enable post-hoc DP auditing and improve lower bounds on privacy parameters relative to the original Steinke-style audit setup, while reducing the number of required canaries. For dataset inference, the method detects membership of diverse data subsets containing NIDs and reports no false positives in the controlled experiments. The approach depends on having enough naturally occurring NIDs, an accurate generator for their format, and a measurable memorization signal in the audited model.","reading_guide":"Skim time: about 25–35 minutes. Read the introduction for the audit setting and the distinction between DP auditing, membership inference, and dataset inference. Then inspect the NID definition and the conditions under which generated strings are valid IID non-members. The most useful experimental details are the Pythia/Pile and OLMo/Dolma setups, the false-positive analysis, and the sample-complexity comparison against canary-based auditing. For pretraining data governance, focus on the NID taxonomy and the dataset inference experiments.","authors_note":"CISPA Helmholtz Center for Information Security; Lorenzo Rossi, Bartłomiej Marek, Franziska Boenisch, and Adam Dziedzic."},"recommendation":{"zh":"读方法和误报分析；NID 是少数能落到真实预训练数据审计的信号，但 tokenizer/去重会决定上限。","en":"Read the method and false-positive analysis; NIDs are a practical audit signal, but tokenizer behavior and deduplication will set the ceiling."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Lorenzo Rossi","Bartłomiej Marek","Franziska Boenisch","Adam Dziedzic"],"notable_authors":["Adam Dziedzic","Franziska Boenisch"],"tags":["data-auditing","membership-inference","privacy","natural-identifiers","pretraining-data"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24408","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.24408","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.24460","title":"The African Language Tax: Quantifying the Cost, Latency, and Context Penalty of Tokenizing African Languages in Frontier LLMs","abstract":"Commercial large language models bill, scale latency, and budget context per token. Yet tokenizers assign more subword tokens to the same meaning in some languages than in others, so speakers of languages with high token-fertility pay a structural penalty before a model is ever invoked. This penalty is documented for multilingual settings in general, but it has not been measured systematically for African languages at the level of enterprise deployment economics and cognitive context capacity. W","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-23T11:47:03Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2606_24460.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作关注的不是多语言能力本身，而是 tokenizer 在不同语言上的结构性不公平，尤其是非洲语言在前沿 LLM 中被切得更碎，导致同样语义需要更多 token，进而带来更高 API 成本、更长延迟和更短有效上下文。论文要解决的核心问题是：这种“tokenization tax”在非洲语言上到底有多大，能否用严格可复现的并行语料测量方法，把它从抽象讨论变成可量化、可比较、可部署决策使用的指标。","importance":"对预训练研究的价值主要在 tokenizer 和数据分布层面。第一，它提醒人们 tokenizer 不是中性的预处理，而是直接决定训练和推理经济性的建模组件。对基座模型来说，词表学习若长期偏向英语和高资源网页语料，就会把低资源语言的成本永久固化到系统里。第二，论文把 token fertility、context efficiency、成本和延迟连起来，说明 tokenizer 设计会影响真实可用上下文长度，这对长上下文训练、跨语言对齐、统一词表设计都很关键。第三，它提供了一个比较干净的测量框架：用 parallel corpus 固定语义内容，只看语言—tokenizer 对的编码效率差异。这种方法对后续做 multilingual tokenizer、byte-level / unigram / BPE 对比、词表再分配都很有参考价值。","technical_approach":"方法核心是做一个基于并行语料的 tokenizer 审计。作者强调，若直接在非平行语料上比较 token 数，语言差异会和主题、文体、句长混在一起；而并行语料能固定“表达的意义”，使 token 数差异更接近语言形态、书写系统和 tokenizer 词表覆盖本身。为此，论文对 20 种非洲语言和 11 个当前前沿/开放 tokenizer 做统一测量。\n\n指标设计比较扎实。基础计数包括统一 Unicode NFC 归一化后的 word count、token count、character count、UTF-8 byte count。token count 明确排除 special/BOS/EOS，避免固定序列开销污染比较。word segmentation 使用统一的 Unicode UAX-29 规则，而不是为某些语言手工定制，这样牺牲了一部分语言学细腻度，但换来跨语言可比性。核心指标有五个：fertility（tokens per word）、premium（相对英语的 fertility 倍数）、characters per token、bytes per token，以及固定窗口下的 context efficiency。论文还明确采用 corpus-level sum-then-divide，而不是 sentence-level mean-of-ratios，避免短句比例过高时统计不稳定。\n\n在结果解释层，作者没有停留在 tokenizer 内部指标，而是把 fertility 映射到企业部署关心的量：美元和本地货币成本、生成延迟倍数、固定 context window 下可容纳真实内容的比例。这里的思路很实用：如果某语言的 fertility 是英语的 P 倍，那么在相同 API 定价和上下文窗口下，它天然要付出 P 倍左右的 token 成本，并损失相应的“操作记忆”。论文还把汇率波动纳入本地货币成本模型，这一点对非洲市场是现实问题，不只是学术装饰。","experiments":"从全文可确认的实验设计看，作者评估了 20 种非洲语言、11 个 tokenizer，并给出 corpus-level aggregation 与 bootstrap confidence intervals。语言覆盖多种脚本和形态类型，包括拉丁、Geʿez/Ethiopic、Arabic/Ajami、N’Ko 等，这使得结果不只是“低资源语言更差”这么简单，而能分离脚本和形态复杂度的影响。论文还把结果实例化到三个部署场景：银行客服、临床分诊、农业短信咨询，用来展示 fertility 差异如何转化为实际预算、延迟和上下文损耗。\n\n不过，就目前给出的全文片段，具体每种语言和每个 tokenizer 的主结果数值、最高 premium、不同 tokenizer 排名差异等细节没有完整展开，因此没法负责任地复述精确榜单数字。可以确定的是，作者的目标不是证明某一个 tokenizer 最好，而是建立一个可复现基线，并展示非洲语言的 tokenization penalty 明显高于英语，且这种惩罚会同时体现在成本、延迟和 context erosion 上。若阅读全文，最值得抓的应是各语言在不同 tokenizer 下的 premium 分布、脚本族之间的系统差异，以及 context efficiency 在 128k 窗口下的相对损失。","reading_guide":"泛读时先看 Introduction 和 Definitions & Metrics，这两部分决定了论文是否站得住。重点确认三件事：为什么必须用 parallel corpus；为什么 fertility 要用 sum-then-divide 聚合；为什么要同时报告 bytes/token 和 context efficiency，而不只看 tokens/word。接着看主结果表和 leaderboard 式图，关注哪些 tokenizer 在非洲语言上更稳，哪些脚本族受到的惩罚最大。最后看 cost model 和三个 deployment scenario，判断这些数字是否足够接近真实生产约束。\n\n预计 25–35 分钟可以读完主线。若想深挖，建议重点看附录里的语料来源、分词实现、异常样本处理和 bootstrap 细节，因为这类测量论文最容易在预处理上引入偏差。另一个值得追问的问题是：如果某些语言在 word The observation is simple in form: the training loss $L$ decreases predictably as we scale up model size $N$, dataset size $D$, and compute $C$, following a power-law curve, which appears as a straight line on a log-log plot. We can view scaling laws as a framework for describing the relationship between compute, loss, model size and data; at its core, it is about how to allocate precious compute optimally between ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"blog","sources":["blog"],"published_at":"2026-06-24T00:00:00+00:00","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/blog-lilian-weng-d01eba4a.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":[],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇长文要解决的不是提出一个新 scaling law，而是把语言模型 scaling law 里最容易被误读的几件事讲清楚：一，Kaplan 与 Chinchilla 为什么会得出不同的 compute-optimal 结论；二，现实里应该怎样拟合 loss–model–data–compute 的关系，哪些拟合方法会系统性地产生偏差；三，power law 现象到底是在什么区间内成立，什么时候会因为数据不足、不可约误差或实验设计问题而失真。全文的重点不是复述结论，而是解释“为什么同样看起来合理的实验流程，会把最优参数/最优 token 配比估错很多”。","importance":"对预训练研究的价值主要在方法论层面。它把 scaling law 从一句经验口号，拆成了可操作的实验设计问题：如何选点、如何控制 token budget、如何做 IsoFLOP 曲线、如何做参数化拟合、如何识别 data-limited 区域、如何避免把未收敛训练误当成 compute-optimal 规律。对做大模型配比、token 预算、mid-train 继续训练、数据再利用的人，这类澄清比再看一遍 Kaplan 或 Chinchilla 原文更直接。贡献量级不是“提出新定律”，而是把已有定律的适用条件、冲突来源和工程陷阱讲透，属于高质量研究笔记。","technical_approach":"文章先从更早的学习曲线工作讲起，包括 Amari、Hestness、Rosenfeld 等，目的是说明 power law 并不是 LLM 时代突然出现的现象，而是更一般的误差—容量—数据关系在现代深度学习中的经验化表达。随后进入 Kaplan 体系：分别考察 test loss 对参数量 N、数据量 D、计算量 C 的幂律关系，并强调联合形式里过拟合程度主要由 N^(alpha/beta)/D 这样的比例控制。这一步的关键是，Kaplan 的结论建立在当时的实验覆盖范围和拟合方式上，因此会把 compute-optimal 点推向“更大的模型、相对更少的 token”。\n\n接着文章详细拆解 Chinchilla 的三种方法。Method 1 是固定模型大小、扫描 token budget，看每个模型在不同训练 token 下的最优点；Method 2 是 IsoFLOP profiles，在固定总计算预算下比较不同 N–D 组合；Method 3 是直接对联合损失函数做参数化拟合。作者强调，三种方法并不是简单替代关系，而是各自对不同误差源敏感：固定模型扫 token 更容易看清 undertraining；IsoFLOP 更贴近真实 compute allocation；参数化拟合最省实验，但对函数形式和采样区域很敏感。\n\n文章一个很有用的部分是“现实中的拟合陷阱”。它指出，如果实验点主要落在 data-infinite 或 model-limited 的局部区域，拟合出来的指数会带明显偏差；如果把尚未充分训练的点混入拟合，或者用过窄的模型/数据范围外推，最后得到的 compute-optimal 配比会系统性偏向大模型欠训练方案。文中用 toy simulation 说明：即便底层真实规律已知，只要采样区域和噪声结构不合适，拟合出的指数也会稳定地错。这个分析解释了为什么 Kaplan 与 Chinchilla 的差异不只是“新论文推翻旧论文”，而是实验设计与拟合视角不同导致的估计偏移。\n\n最后，文章讨论为什么会出现 power law。这里没有给出严格理论证明，而是把它放回更一般的统计学习与经验现象中：在远离饱和区和极小规模区时，很多误差项会表现出近似尺度不变性，因此在 log-log 坐标下呈线性。这个部分更像解释框架，而不是封闭理论。","experiments":"这不是原始实验论文，重点不在发布新 benchmark 数字，而在复盘已有结果与拟合方法。文中回顾了 Kaplan 2020 的关键实验设定：Transformer LM，模型规模约从 7.68 亿到 15 亿非 embedding 参数，数据规模从 2200 万到 230 亿 token，使用 3000 step warmup 加 cosine decay。Kaplan 的核心结论之一是 compute-optimal 下 N_opt ∝ C^0.73，因此 10 倍算力应把模型放大约 5.5 倍、token 只增约 1.8 倍。文章指出这正是后来被 Chinchilla 修正的地方，因为该指数会导致模型明显欠训练。\n\n对 Chinchilla 部分，文章强调其结论是更接近“参数量与训练 token 近似同阶增长”的 compute-optimal 配比，也就是在固定算力下应训练更小但更充分的数据覆盖模型。虽然这里给出的全文摘录没有展开全部具体数字，但核心实验洞察很明确：当你真正扫描 token budget 或做 IsoFLOP 比较时，很多 Kaplan 风格的大模型点并没有训练到合适区域，因此最优点被高估到过大的 N。\n\n最有信息量的“实验”其实是 toy simulation。它展示了拟合流程本身会制造偏差：即使真实损失函数已知，只要观测点集中在局部区间、噪声存在、或拟合形式过强，外推出的 scaling exponent 和最优配比都可能明显偏离真实值。对做内部 scaling study 的团队，这个结论比单个指数更重要。","reading_guide":"泛读时建议抓四块内容。第一块是 Kaplan 与 Chinchilla 两节，先看它们各自到底测了什么、最优点是怎么定义的。第二块是 Chinchilla 的三种方法，尤其是 Method 1 和 Method 2，这两部分最接近实际做配比实验时的决策流程。第三块是“Trickiness of Fitting Scaling Laws in Reality”和 toy simulation，这部分最值得看，因为它直接对应工程里最常见的误判来源。第四块是 Why power law，可快速浏览，把它当作背景解释即可。\n\n如果已经熟悉 Kaplan/Chinchilla 原文，预计 20–30 分钟可以抓住主要信息；如果正在设计新的 scaling 实验，建议把拟合陷阱和采样区间分析细看一遍，并对照自己当前的 sweep 设计检查是否存在 data-limited、undertrained 或外推过远的问题。","authors_note":"Lilian Weng，OpenAI。长期写作与整理大模型训练、对齐、agent、scaling It is clarifying three things that are easy to get wrong in practice: why Kaplan and Chinchilla reached different compute-optimal prescriptions, how scaling-law fitting should actually be done under realistic constraints, and where the apparent power-law regime breaks because of data limitation, irreducible error, or flawed experimental design. The central question is: how can seemingly reasonable small-scale studies produce badly biased recommendations for model/data allocation at large scale?","importance":"Its value is methodological rather than algorithmic. It turns scaling laws from a slogan into an experimental discipline: how to choose sweep points, how to vary token budgets, how to use IsoFLOP profiles, how to fit joint loss surfaces, and how to detect when a regime is data-limited or undertrained. For pretraining researchers working on compute allocation, token budgeting, continued pretraining, or data reuse, this is more useful than a surface-level recap of Kaplan or Chinchilla. The contribution is not a new law, but a careful account of applicability, failure modes, and fitting bias.","technical_approach":"$19","experiments":"$1a","reading_guide":"For a skim, focus on four parts. (3) Plasticity loss occurs even under stationary multilingual training without abrupt task switches, showing that sharp distribution shifts, a common setup in prior plasticity research, are not a necessary trigger for the phenomenon. The answer is no. Top-1 concentration measures token-side prediction saturation, and in these DLM runs it is already high before meaningful optimization begins. It quickly loses sensitivity to downstream training stability. The architecture stacks three recurrent levels at progressively slower timescales; each level receives the prediction error of the level below as input, rather than its raw hidden state. On enwiki8 with equal token budgets, Harmonic outperforms a comparable Transformer (28M params) by +1.4% at 1K tokens, +6.7% at 8K tokens, and +11.4% at 32K tokens (bpt, lower is better). Under RoPE, however, a key's contribution to a future attention logit decomposes into a position-dependent sum over two-dimensional frequency blocks. This makes key-cache quantization a block-wise bit-allocation problem: high-energy RoPE blocks are more sensitive to quantization error and should receive more bits. We introduce Block-GTQ, a RoPE-aware bit allocator for key-cache quantization built on TurboQuant-MSE","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-23T00:17:48Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2606_24033.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是长上下文推理里一个很具体但常被粗糙处理的问题：RoPE 模型中的 key cache 量化，不应被看成普通向量压缩，而应被看成“对未来 attention logit 影响不同的频率块之间如何分配比特”的问题。现有低比特 KV-cache 方法大多把每个 key 当成平坦向量，优化重建误差；但在 RoPE 下，query-key logit 是按 2 维旋转频率块分解的，不同 block 对未来 logits 的敏感度并不一样。结果是，统一 bit allocation 会把预算浪费在低影响 block 上，同时压坏真正决定注意力分布的高能量 block，尤其在长上下文和推理任务上会出现性能塌陷。","importance":"对基座模型研究的意义主要不在“又一个量化技巧”，而在于它把 KV-cache 压缩目标重新对齐到了模型真正使用 key 的方式上：不是最小化向量 MSE，而是更接近最小化 RoPE attention logit 误差。这种目标重写很有启发性，因为它说明推理侧压缩如果忽略预训练时引入的结构性归纳偏置，往往会在表面压缩率相同的情况下造成完全不同的功能退化。对长上下文 serving 来说，这项工作给出了一条很实用的路线：在不改模型、不引入 recent buffer 依赖的前提下，仅通过更合理的 bit allocation 就能显著改善低比特 K-cache 的可用性。对预训练研究员而言，更重要的启发是：RoPE 不是“后处理细节”，它改变了表示的误差几何，任何压缩、蒸馏、甚至训练时正则都可能需要围绕这种 block structure 重新设计。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 Block-GTQ。它建立在 TurboQuant-MSE 之上，但不再对整个 key head 统一分配 bit，而是对每个 layer、每个 KV head 内的 RoPE 2D block 做整数 bit allocation。论文先从 RoPE attention 写出 logit 形式：对于相对位置 Δ，logit 是 q^T R_Δ k，可拆成多个 2×2 旋转块项之和。于是 key 的量化误差对 logit 的影响天然是 block-wise 的。基于这个观察，作者为每个 block 构造了一个无需标签的 energy score，来自 Q/K 激活统计，用来近似该 block 对未来 query-key 信号的重要性。然后结合 TQ-MSE 的经验误差率律 4^{-b}，把“给某个 block 多 1 bit 能减少多少预期误差”写成边际收益，并在固定平均 bit budget 下做 greedy integer allocation。分到相同比特宽度的 block 再复用原始 TQ-MSE 的局部编码器，因此实现代价不高。值向量 V 没有参与 RoPE key-logit 计算，所以仍然采用 uniform TQ-MSE。这个设计很克制：它不改 attention，不改 cache layout 的语义，只改 K 的 bit 分配策略。论文还实现了 packed-cache serving path，把压缩后的 K/V code 直接用于推理路径，而不是只做离线误差分析。","experiments":"实验分三层。第一层是机制验证：在 10 个模型的诊断面板上，作者比较了相同 K-only bit budget 下 Block-GTQ 与 uniform TQ-MSE 的 RoPE-logit 保真度。在 2 b/dim 和 3 b/dim 两个预算下，per-layer RoPE-logit MAE 相对下降 32% 到 80%，并且在 367/367 个 layer comparison 上全部胜出，说明收益不是少数层驱动，而是相当稳定的结构性改进。第二层是长上下文任务：在 Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct 上，K2V2 预算下，六任务 NIAH 平均分从 70.6 提升到 97.4；八任务 LongBench-EN 平均分从 36.87 提升到 53.31。这个幅度已经说明 uniform K quantization 在低比特下不是“略差”，而是会明显破坏可用性。第三层是推理与数学任务鲁棒性：在 DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B 上，AIME 2024/2025 的 K3V2 设置里，Block-GTQ 达到 51.7/37.5，接近 fp16 的 54.2/37.9，而 uniform TQ-MSE 直接掉到 0.0/0.0。这个结果很关键，因为它说明低比特 key 误差会通过 attention 分布放大，最终把长链推理彻底打坏；而 RoPE-aware allocation 可以显著缓解。系统侧，在 Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct 上用 K3V3 Block-GTQ 的 packed path，相比 fp16 FlashAttention2，128K context 下 KV cache 压缩 3.24×，峰值显存从 56.31GB 降到 19.85GB，单请求 decode latency 从 70.96ms 降到 52.95ms；256K 和 512K 时 fp16 baseline 已 OOM，而压缩路径仍可运行，峰值显存分别为 33.42GB 和 60.56GB。","reading_guide":"泛读时建议先抓三处。第一，看引言和 RoPE-structured key-cache error 的推导，确认作者为什么坚持“key 量化目标应是 logit preservation 而不是 vector reconstruction”。这是整篇文章最有信息量的地方。第二，看 bit allocation 的公式化部分，重点理解 energy score 的定义、为什么能做到 label-free、以及 4^{-b} 误差率律如何把 allocation 变成 greedy marginal gain 问题。第三，看实验里 AIME 和 LongBench/NIAH 的结果表，这些比单纯 logit MAE 更能说明方法是否真的保住了模型功能。若时间有限，优先读方法章节前半、主结果表、以及 packed serving 的实现说明，预计 25 到 35 分钟可以抓住主要贡献。更值得深挖的点有两个：一是 energy profile 是否与预训练阶段不同层的频率利用模式有关；二是这种 block-aware 思路能否迁移到训练时的表示正则、蒸馏损失或 tokenizer 设计上。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a specific but important mismatch in long-context inference: key-cache quantization in RoPE-based models should not be treated as flat vector compression. Under RoPE, a future query-key logit decomposes into a sum over 2D frequency blocks, and different blocks contribute very differently to the final attention score. Existing low-bit KV-cache methods mostly optimize reconstruction error on the key vector itself, which ignores this structured sensitivity. As a result, uniform bit allocation can waste precision on low-impact blocks while under-protecting the blocks that actually control attention logits, leading to severe degradation on long-context and reasoning tasks.","importance":"The main value is not just a better quantizer. The paper reframes KV-cache compression around the quantity the model actually uses: RoPE attention logits rather than vector MSE. That shift is useful for foundation-model researchers because it shows how inference-time compression can fail when it ignores the representational geometry induced by pretraining choices such as RoPE. In practical serving, the method shows that one can recover much of the utility of low-bit key caches without changing the model or relying on an fp16 recent-key buffer. More broadly, it suggests that RoPE is not a superficial implementation detail; it changes how errors propagate, so compression, distillation, and possibly even training-time regularization may need to respect the same block structure.","technical_approach":"$1d","experiments":"$1e","reading_guide":"$1f","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"RoPE频率块特性是预训练架构设计的核心细节，重点看§3的块能量计算逻辑，可反哺预训练时的RoPE超参与维度分配。","en":"RoPE frequency block characteristics are a core pre-training architecture detail; focus on §3's block energy calculation logic, which can inform RoPE hyperparameter and dimension allocation during pre-training."},"metadata":{"institutions":["The Chinese University of Hong Kong"],"authors":["Fengfeng Liang","Yuechen Zhang","Jiaya Jia"],"notable_authors":["Jiaya Jia"],"tags":["kv-cache quantization","RoPE","inference efficiency","long-context inference","bit allocation"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24033","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.24033","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.24447","title":"P-MTP: Efficient Document Parsing via Multi-Token Prediction with Progressive Depth Scaling","abstract":"Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have revolutionized document parsing by enabling end-to-end mapping from images to structured text, imposing a significant latency bottleneck, particularly for token-dense documents. VLM 的一个很具体但也很现实的问题：输出序列很长，标准自回归解码一步只出一个 token，延迟高；而直接把 LLM 里的 multi-token prediction 套到文档解析上，又会在 look-ahead 深度变大时出现训练不稳和推理接受率下降。更细一点看，问题不只是“多预测几个 token 难”，而是远端 supervision 会在近端 token 已经错掉时继续回传梯度，造成错误轨迹上的噪声学习；同时固定深度 speculative drafting 也无法适应文档中不同区域的难度差异，简单区域浪费潜在加速，复杂区域则频繁 reject。论文的目标就是让 MTP 在文档解析这种 token-dense、视觉证据强约束的场景里，能把 look-ahead 5×。作者把这个结果归因于两点同时成立：训练阶段更深的 look-ahead 可以稳定学出来，推理阶段动态 drafting 又能把简单样本上的潜在接受长度真正兑现出来。\n\n实验结构上，值得注意的应当包括几类。第一类是与标准 NTP、自带固定深度 MTP、不同静态 loss weighting 策略的对比，用来证明 progressive weighting 的必要性。第二类是 look-ahead 深度 K 的扩展实验，通常会展示随着 K 增大，基线方法 acceptance rate 明显下降，而 P-MTP 能把更深 K 维持在可用区间。第三类是动态 drafting 的消融，验证固定 K 即便训练得住，也会因为场景难度波动而损失 αK。第四类是模块开销分析，轻量 MTP 分支的额外 overhead γ 需要足够低，否则理论 speedup 不能转成 wall-clock gain。\n\n目前可从正文直接提取的硬数字主要是“up to 5× speedup with negligible impact on parsing accuracy”。摘要截断，没有完整 benchmark 表、参数量、训练数据规模、具体 acceptance rate 和不同 K 下的曲线数字。如果精读原文，建议重点补齐：1）主干 VLM 规模；2）MTP bottleneck in document-parsing VLMs: decoding is slow because outputs are long and standard autoregressive generation emits one token per step. Multi-token prediction is an obvious acceleration route, but pushing the look-ahead depth deeper causes two coupled failures in this setting. During training, distal supervision becomes noisy once proximal predictions have already diverged, so deeper targets inject gradients along unreliable trajectories. During inference, a fixed drafting depth is a poor fit because document regions vary a lot in local difficulty: easy spans could support deeper drafting, while hard spans trigger frequent rejection. The paper tries to make deep MTP actually usable for token-dense document parsing, not just theoretically appealing.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense, but it is relevant to pretraining researchers because it isolates a general optimization issue: how to supervise multi-step predictions when later targets are only meaningful if earlier ones remain reliable. That same issue appears in long-horizon target design, speculative objectives, and broader non-standard LM training setups. A second useful point is that the paper treats training and inference as a coupled system: better acceptance at inference requires a trajectory-aware loss during training, not only a decoding heuristic. The contribution is best read as a solid adaptation of MTP to a high-value structured generation setting, with lessons that may transfer to code generation, OCR-heavy generation, and other high-confidence token prediction regimes.","technical_approach":"$21","experiments":"$22","reading_guide":"$23","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"将 MTP 扩展到更深深度的训练 trick，其 Progressive Curriculum Loss 对稳定 MTP 训练有启发，值得看方法部分。","en":"Training tricks for scaling MTP to deeper depths; its Progressive Curriculum Loss is instructive for stabilizing MTP training. Weights are stable and model-specific, while KV-cache is transient and demand-driven. Reserving peak KV capacity per model wastes memory, but a monolithic memory pool still lets static weights compete with dynamic KV-cache and can limit usable long-context capacity under low-concurrency traffic.","importance":"The pretraining relevance is indirect but concrete. Architectural choices such as MoE, MQA, MLA, and long-context support are not only training-time efficiency decisions; they also determine how the model behaves under multi-model serving. CrossPool shows that low-KV-head attention can reduce per-token KV memory while still creating capacity-exposure problems when replicas are cold and requests are sparse. This is useful feedback for architecture teams that care about the full train-to-serve lifecycle.","technical_approach":"CrossPool disaggregates FFN weights and KV-cache into separate GPU memory pools. Since MoE FFNs dominate parameter storage, their weights are consolidated in a weight pool. Attention and other non-FFN modules stay in the KV-cache pool so that KV access remains local. The boundary exchanges hidden states instead of KV-cache tensors, avoiding expensive KV movement but introducing cross-pool scheduling and communication challenges. The system adds an offline KV-cache planner with virtualized paging, a layer-wise pipeline scheduler that overlaps hidden-state transfers with computation, and persistent GPU-resident control kernels to reduce graph launch and scheduling overhead.","experiments":"The evaluation compares CrossPool with Static Partition and kvcached on a production-grade serving platform. The reported finding is that CrossPool improves memory pooling for cold MoE workloads, enables longer-context requests under the same hardware budget, and keeps competitive performance on more balanced workloads. The motivation section also reports a highly skewed OpenRouter model-demand distribution, with roughly 90% of models falling into the cold category.
